(40)

Proof.

These statements follow from the 2-complex properties of
Theorem
2.4
and elementary manipulations with
first-order differential operators. For instance, to prove the last,

\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\circ\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}w=-\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}(\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{grad}w){\mathbbm{i}})=-\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{mskw}\operatorname{grad}(\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{grad}w))=0,
where we
have used (
26b
); and of course,

\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\circ\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}
must vanish due to the 2-complex
property of Theorem
2.4
.
∎

3.
Regular decompositions, density, and continuous right inverses

Right inverses of exterior derivatives that are continuous in
appropriate Sobolev norms were given in
[
16
]
, inspired
by the classical work of
[
10
]
. In this section, we leverage
their results to show regular decompositions of some Sobolev spaces of
matrix fields, prove density of smooth functions in them, and
construct right inverses of the differential operators
in (
37
).

in (

are the subscripted

and

(41)

The downward arrows in (
41
) can also be provided
with corresponding upward right inverses. They are not marked to
reduce clutter and because the same information is contained in the
horizontal arrows back and forth. Below, we detail the construction
of each new right inverse operator. Note that some of the
D
and
R

operators map from and into
subspaces
of the respective domains
and codomains indicated in (
41
). The codomain
subspaces consist of (more)
regular
functions. The domain
subspaces are
kernels
of one of the (multiple) operators acting
on the space. These subspaces are given precisely in the case by case results
below (and summarized in Theorem
3.21
). Throughout, we denote the null space of a linear operator
A:X\to Y

by

\ker(A:X).

Note, e.g.,
\ker(\operatorname{curl}:{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl}))
is different
from
\ker(\operatorname{curl}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}})=\{g\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}:\operatorname{curl}g=0\}
.

Regular decompositions for standard Sobolev spaces based on the
exterior derivative can be inferred from the results
of
[
16
]
. In this section, we also show how to combine
their results with our previous results to produce regular
decompositions for the following new spaces of matrix fields:

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}

\displaystyle=\{g\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}:\operatorname{curl}g\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\operatorname{inc}g\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}\},

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}

\displaystyle=\{\tau\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}:\operatorname{div}\tau\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\tau\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\},

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}

\displaystyle=\{\sigma\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}:\operatorname{div}\sigma\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\}.

They are normed, respectively, by

\|\cdot\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}}
, the
norms defined in (
23
). Obviously, the spaces
defined in (
24
) are subspaces of these spaces,
i.e.,

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}\subseteq\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}},\quad\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}\subseteq\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}},\quad\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}\subseteq\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}.

(42)

The theorems in this section (Theorems
3.4
,
3.7
, and
3.10
) improve these inclusions to equalities,
thus also proving the density of their respective subspaces of
compactly supported smooth functions.

Our results are under the additional
assumptions on
\varOmega
that it is simply connected and
that its boundary is connected. Then the topology of
\varOmega
is trivial.
Covering
\varOmega
by subdomains starlike with respect to a ball and using
regularized Bogovskiĭ operators in each subdomain,

[
16
, Theorem 4.9]
proves that there exist continuous
linear operators

\displaystyle{{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\to\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+1}},\qquad{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\to\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+1}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\qquad{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}\to\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+1}},

(43a)

satisfying

\displaystyle\operatorname{grad}({{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}v)

\displaystyle=v\qquad\text{ for all }v\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\text{ with }\operatorname{curl}v=0,

(43b)

\displaystyle\operatorname{curl}({{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}q)

\displaystyle=q\qquad\text{ for all }q\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\text{ with }\operatorname{div}q=0,

(43c)

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}({{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}u)

\displaystyle=u\qquad\text{ for all }u\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}\text{ with }u(1)=0,

(43d)

for any real number
s
, where
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}
is the subspace of
distributions on
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
defined in (
17
). The last condition
in (
43d
)
is a zero mean condition on
u
given through a functional action that makes sense even for negative
s
. For
s\geq 0
, it can be expressed using the
L_{2}
inner product as
(u,1)=0
.

It is standard to use (
43
) to produce regular
decompositions of
{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
and
{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})
. Indeed, any

u\in{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
can be decomposed into

u=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u+\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u,\quad\text{ with }\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u={T}_{\mathrm{c}}\operatorname{curl}u,\;\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u={T}_{\mathrm{g}}(u-{T}_{\mathrm{c}}\operatorname{curl}u),

(44)

as can be immediately verified using (
43c
)
and (
43b
). By the continuity properties of
{T}_{\mathrm{g}}
and
{T}_{\mathrm{c}}
,
the operators
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}:{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
and
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}:{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}
are continuous. Since
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u
and
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u
have

{\mathring{H}}^{1}
-regularity (higher than what may be expected of
u
), this is
referred to as a “regular decomposition” of
{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
. The process of arriving
at this decomposition can be viewed as first generating a zero curl
function
u-{T}_{\mathrm{c}}\operatorname{curl}u
and then moving
left
of
{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})

in the diagram (
41
) to create a potential in

{\mathring{H}}^{1}
using the operator
{T}_{\mathrm{g}}
. For the matrix-valued function
spaces in the middle of (
41
), the process is
similar, but we have more options to move, such as
up, left, or
diagonally,
and our regular decompositions that follow have
multiple potentials.

3.1.
Regular decomposition of
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}

We start with a result that can also be found in
[
2
, Theorem 2]
as a special case of existence of regular potential. Here we provide an explicit construction (see also
[
11
]
).

Lemma 3.1
.

There is a linear map

\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cc}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}})\to\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+2}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
such that

\sigma=\operatorname{inc}{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cc}}}}\sigma,\qquad\|{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cc}}}}\sigma\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+2}}}\lesssim\|\sigma\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}}.

for any
s\in\mathbb{{R}}
.

Proof.

Let
\sigma\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
and
\operatorname{div}\sigma=0
. Applying
{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}
to row
vectors of
\sigma
, whose components are all distributions in

\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
supported on
\bar{\varOmega}
per (
17
), we find
from (
43c
) that there is a

\eta\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+1}}\otimes\mathbb{{M}}
such that the identity

\sigma=\operatorname{curl}\eta

holds in
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
. Also, since
\sigma
is symmetric,

\operatorname{skw}\sigma=0=\operatorname{skw}\operatorname{curl}\eta=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{mskw}\operatorname{div}S\eta

by (
26d
). Hence

S\eta\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+1}}\otimes\mathbb{{M}}
has vanishing divergence in
all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
(and obviously
S\eta
is supported on
\bar{\varOmega}
). Applying
{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}
row-wise to
S\eta
, we conclude
from (
43c
) that there is a

\gamma\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+2}}\otimes\mathbb{{M}}
such that

S\eta=\operatorname{curl}\gamma.

Set
g=\operatorname{sym}\gamma
in

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+2}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
. Then

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}\eta=\operatorname{curl}S^{-1}\operatorname{curl}\gamma

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}S^{-1}\operatorname{curl}(\operatorname{skw}\gamma+g)=\operatorname{inc}g

where the last equality is due to (
26e
) and (
26f
).
By the continuity of

{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}
, the linear map
\sigma\mapsto g
we just constructed is
continuous and is the needed map
{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cc}}}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.2
.

There is a linear map
{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{inc}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}})\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
such
that for any
g\in\ker(\operatorname{inc}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}})
,

\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}g=\operatorname{curl}g,\qquad\|{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}g\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\operatorname{curl}g\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}.

Proof.

Given any
g\in\ker(\operatorname{inc}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}})
, since

\operatorname{inc}g=\operatorname{curl}(\T\operatorname{curl}g)=0
, applying
{{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}
to each row vector
of
\T\operatorname{curl}g
in
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
and
using (
43b
), we obtain a

q\in L_{2}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
satisfying

\T\operatorname{curl}g=\operatorname{grad}q

on all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
. Moreover, by (
26f
),
\operatorname{curl}g
has
zero trace, so

0=\operatorname{tr}(\T\operatorname{curl}g)=\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{grad}q)=\operatorname{div}q.

Hence
u=\frac{1}{2}{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}q
is in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
and satisfies

q=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}u
. Therefore, using (
31
),

\displaystyle\operatorname{curl}g

\displaystyle=\T\operatorname{grad}q=\frac{1}{2}\T\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{curl}u=\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}u.

Denoting the linear map
g\mapsto u
by
{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}
, the proof is
now completed using the continuity of
{{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}
and
{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.3
.

There is a linear map

\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gg}}}:\ker(\operatorname{curl}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}})\to\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+2}}
such that

g=\operatorname{hess}\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gg}}}g,\qquad\|\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gg}}}g\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+2}}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}}.

for any
s\in\mathbb{{R}}
and
g\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{{s}}}
with vanishing curl.

Proof.

Let
g\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}
have zero curl. Then applying
{{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}
to the row
vectors of
g
and using (
43b
), we obtain a

u\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+1}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
, supported on
\bar{\varOmega}
, such that
the identity

g=\operatorname{grad}u

holds on all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
. Applying
\operatorname{sym}
to both sides,

g=\operatorname{def}u.

A further application of
\operatorname{curl}
on both sides
yields

0=\operatorname{curl}g=\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}u=\frac{1}{2}\T\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{curl}u,

by (
31
), i.e., all first order derivatives of
\operatorname{curl}u
vanish.
Hence there must
exist a constant vector
b\in\mathbb{{V}}
such that
\operatorname{curl}u=b

holds on all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
. But
u
is supported on
\bar{\varOmega}
, so
b

must be the zero vector. Now that we have shown
\operatorname{curl}u=0
,
putting
w={{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}u
and using (
43b
), we
find that
w\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+2}}
satisfies
\operatorname{grad}w=u
and

\displaystyle\operatorname{hess}(w)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{def}(\operatorname{grad}w)=\operatorname{def}(u)=g.

The linear map
g\mapsto w
we just constructed is the needed operator
\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gg}}}
.
∎

Theorem 3.4
.

There exist three continuous linear operators

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\qquad\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\qquad\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1},

such that any
g\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}
can be decomposed into

g=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{hess}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g.

(45)

Consequently,
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}=\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
.

Proof.

Put
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g:={\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cc}}}}\operatorname{inc}g
. By Lemma
3.1
,

\operatorname{inc}(g-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g)=0.

Consequently, by Lemma
3.2
,

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g:={\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}(g-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g)
is in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}

satisfies

\operatorname{curl}\big(g-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g-\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g\big)=0.

(46)

Applying Lemma
3.3
with
s=-1
, we find that

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g:=\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gg}}}(g-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g-\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g)
satisfies

g-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g-\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g=\operatorname{hess}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g,

and has the required continuity property, thus completing the proof
of
45
.

To prove that
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}=\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
, in view of
(
42
), it suffices to prove that
any
g\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}
,
decomposed as above into
g=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{hess}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g
,
is in
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
. By the density of
\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)
in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}(\varOmega)
,
there are

g_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
,

u_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
,
and

w_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)
such that

\|g_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\qquad\|u_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\qquad\|w_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,

as
m\to\infty
. Hence, by (
18
),

g_{m}+\operatorname{def}u_{m}+\operatorname{hess}w_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
converges
to
g
in
\|\cdot\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}}
-norm, proving that
g\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
.
∎

3.2.
Regular decomposition of
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}

Lemma 3.5
.

There is a linear map

{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{dd}}}}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}\to\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+2}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
such
that for any
s\in\mathbb{{R}}
and
w\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}
,

\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{dd}}}}w=w,\qquad\|{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{dd}}}}w\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+2}}}\lesssim\|w\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}}.

(47)

Proof.

Let
w\in\mathcal{{D}}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}
. Since
(w,1)=0
,
by (
43d
),
q={{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w
satisfies

\operatorname{div}q=w,\qquad\|q\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+1}}}\lesssim\|w\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s}}}.

Since
q
is supported on
\bar{\varOmega}
,
we may integrate by parts to see that

0=(w,p_{1})=(\operatorname{div}q,p_{1})=-(q,\operatorname{grad}p_{1})
for any
p_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
. Thus all
components of
q
have zero mean on

\varOmega
. Applying (
43d
) again, we then obtain a

\tau\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{s+2}}\otimes\mathbb{{M}}
such that
\operatorname{div}\tau=q
. Let

u=\operatorname{vskw}\tau
. Then
\operatorname{div}\operatorname{skw}\tau=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{mskw}u=-\operatorname{curl}u

by (
26a
). Collecting these observations, and putting

\sigma=\operatorname{sym}\tau
,

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}q=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\tau=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}(\operatorname{sym}\tau+\operatorname{skw}\tau)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma-\operatorname{div}\operatorname{curl}u=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma.

Denote the linear map
w\mapsto\sigma
we just constructed by

{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{dd}}}}w
. By the continuity of
{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}
, we see that
{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{dd}}}}
satisfies the
norm estimate in (
47
) for all
w\in\mathcal{{D}}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}
. Hence
by the density result of Lemma
2.2
,
{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{dd}}}}
has a
unique continuous extension, which is the required map.
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

There is a linear map

{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}})\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}

such that for all

\sigma\in\ker(\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}),

\operatorname{div}\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}\sigma=\operatorname{div}\sigma,\qquad\|{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}\sigma\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\operatorname{div}\sigma\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}.

Proof.

Consider a
\sigma\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}
with
\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma=0
. Then, since

\operatorname{div}\sigma\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
has vanishing divergence,

u={{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}\operatorname{div}\sigma
is in
L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}

and satisfies
\operatorname{curl}u=\operatorname{div}\sigma
by
(
43c
).
Next, we claim that
(u,b)=0
for any
b\in\mathbb{{V}}
. To see this,
first note that the distribution
\operatorname{div}\sigma
satisfies

(\operatorname{div}\sigma)(b\times x)=\sigma(\operatorname{grad}(b\times x))=\sigma(\operatorname{mskw}b)=0

due to the symmetry of
\sigma
.
Relating to
u
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{div}\sigma)(b\times x)=(\operatorname{curl}u)(b\times x)=(u,\operatorname{curl}(b\times x))=2(u,b).

Hence we may apply
{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}
to each component of
\frac{1}{2}u
and
use (
43d
) to get a
\tau\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{M}}

such that
\operatorname{div}\tau=\frac{1}{2}u
, which implies

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}\sigma

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}u=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\operatorname{dev}\tau=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\operatorname{dev}\tau.

Here we have used (
30
) and the fact that
\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}

vanishes on matrix fields that are scalar multiples of the identity.
Denoting the map
\sigma\mapsto\T\operatorname{dev}\tau
by
{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}
, the
continuity of
{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}
and
{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}
finishes the proof.
∎

Theorem 3.7
.

There exist three continuous linear operators

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\qquad\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}},\qquad\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},

such that any
\sigma\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}

can be decomposed into

\sigma=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma+\operatorname{inc}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma.

(48)

Consequently,
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}=\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
.

Proof.

Let
\sigma\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}
and
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma:={\mathop{D_{\mathrm{dd}}}}\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma.

Note that
\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma
is in
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}}
, the domain of

{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{dd}}}}
, because
the hessian of
p
is zero for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
and

(\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma)(p)=\sigma(\operatorname{hess}p)=0.

By
Lemma
3.5
,

\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}(\sigma-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma)=0.

Next, set
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma:={\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}(\sigma-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma)
in

{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
. By Lemma
3.6
,

\operatorname{div}\big(\sigma-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma-\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma\big)=0.

By Lemma
3.1
, setting

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma:={\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cc}}}}(\sigma-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma-\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma)
, we find that

\sigma-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma-\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma=\operatorname{inc}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma,

thus completing the proof of (
48
).

To conclude, it suffices to prove that
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}\subseteq\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
, due
to (
42
). Decompose any

\sigma\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}
into

\sigma=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma+\operatorname{inc}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma
.
By the density of
\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)
in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}
,
there are

\sigma_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
,

\tau_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
,
and

g_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
such that

\|\sigma_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\qquad\|\tau_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\qquad\|g_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,

as
m\to\infty
. Hence, by (
18
),

\sigma_{m}+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\tau_{m}+\operatorname{inc}g_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
converges
to
\sigma
in
\|\cdot\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}}
norm, thus proving that
\sigma\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
.
∎

3.3.
Regular decomposition of
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}

Next, we turn to constructing a regular decomposition of
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
. (The
case of
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}
obviously follows from that of
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
.) Unlike the
three-term decompositions of
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
cases, now we are only
able to construct a decomposition with four terms. We begin with a
preparatory lemma.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
K=\ker(\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}})
.
There are linear maps

{\mathop{\hat{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}:K\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
,

\mathop{\hat{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}:K\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
,
and

\mathop{\hat{U}_{\mathrm{gc}}}:K\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
,
such that any
\tau\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}
with
\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau=0
can be
decomposed into

\tau=\operatorname{curl}{\mathop{\hat{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau+\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}\mathop{\hat{U}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\hat{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau

(49)

and the following continuity bound holds:

\|{\mathop{\hat{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}+\|\mathop{\hat{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}+\|\mathop{\hat{U}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}.

(50)

If in addition,
\tau
is in
L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
, then
\mathop{\hat{U}_{\mathrm{gc}}}
can
be taken to be zero provided (
50
) is replaced by

\|{\mathop{\hat{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}},\qquad\|\mathop{\hat{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{L_{2}}+\|\tau\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}.

Proof.

Given any
\tau\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}
with
\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau=0
, put
w={{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}\operatorname{div}\tau
. Then
by (
43b
),

\operatorname{grad}w=\operatorname{div}\tau,\qquad\|w\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|\operatorname{div}\tau\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}.

(51)

Since
\operatorname{tr}(\T\tau)=0
, we know that
S\T\tau=\tau
, so

\operatorname{skw}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{mskw}\operatorname{div}S\T\tau=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{mskw}\operatorname{div}\tau=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{mskw}\operatorname{grad}w=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}(w{\mathbbm{i}}),

(52)

where we have used (
26d
) and (
26a
).

Let
\sigma=\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau
. By the identity (
30
) of
Lemma
2.1
,

\operatorname{div}\sigma=\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau=0
, so applying
Lemma
3.2
,
g={\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}\sigma
is in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}

and satisfies

\sigma=\operatorname{inc}g,\qquad\|g\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}

(53)

Combined with (
52
), we have the twin identities

\displaystyle\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}\T\operatorname{curl}g,

\displaystyle\operatorname{skw}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}(w{\mathbbm{i}}).

Adding these equations, we find that

\operatorname{curl}(\T\tau-\T\operatorname{curl}g+\frac{1}{2}w{\mathbbm{i}})=0
. Hence,
applying
{{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}
to each of the row vectors of

\T\tau-\T\operatorname{curl}g+\frac{1}{2}w{\mathbbm{i}}
and
using (
43b
), we obtain a
q\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
such that

\displaystyle\operatorname{grad}q=\T\tau-\T\operatorname{curl}g+\frac{1}{2}w{\mathbbm{i}},

(54a)

\displaystyle\|q\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|\tau-\operatorname{curl}g+w\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}.

(54b)

In fact,
q|_{\varOmega}
is in
{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})
. To see this, take traces on
both sides of (
54a
). Recall that
\operatorname{tr}\tau=0
. Also,

\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{curl}g)=0
by (
26f
). Hence we conclude that

\frac{3}{2}w=\operatorname{div}q
, an identity that holds in all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
with

q
and
w
supported only on
\bar{\varOmega}
. Since
w\in L_{2}
, this in
particular shows that
q|_{\varOmega}\in{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})
, and the estimate

\displaystyle\|q\|_{H(\operatorname{div})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}+\|g\|_{H^{1}}+\|w\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}

(55)

follows from the estimates of (
54b
), (
53
),
and (
51
).

Taking the deviatoric part of both sides of (
54a
) and noting
that
\tau=\operatorname{dev}\tau
, we obtain a preliminary two-term
decomposition of
\tau,

\tau=\operatorname{curl}g+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}q.

(56)

However, here
q
is not in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
, in general.
To improve this to the needed result, we use
r={{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}\operatorname{div}q
,
which has the same divergence as
q
, but is in

{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
:

\operatorname{div}r=\operatorname{div}q,\qquad\|r\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\operatorname{div}q\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}},

by (
55
). Since
\operatorname{div}(q-r)=0
, putting

u=\frac{1}{2}{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}(q-r)
in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
,
by (
43c
),

\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}u=q-r,\qquad\|u\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|q-r\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}q=\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}r+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{curl}u=\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}r+\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}u.

Substituting this into (
56
) and setting

{\mathop{\hat{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau=g,\mathop{\hat{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau=r,
and
\mathop{\hat{U}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau=u
, we see
that (
49
) and (
50
)
follow.

To prove the remaining statement, suppose

\tau\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}\cap\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}
. Then, due to the higher
regularity of
\tau
, observe that
q
in (
54a
) is in

{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
and in place of
(
54b
), we have

\|q\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\tau-\operatorname{curl}g+w\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{L_{2}}+\|g\|_{H^{1}}+\|w\|_{L_{2}}.

which can be used in place of (
55
).
There is no
longer a need to produce the
r
above, and we may set

\mathop{\hat{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau=q\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
. The
decomposition (
56
) then concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.9
.

There is a linear map

{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cd}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{{s}}}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}(\operatorname{div}))\to\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{{s+2}}}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}

such that

\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cd}}}}v=v,\qquad\|{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cd}}}}v\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{{s+2}}}}\lesssim\|v\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{{s}}}},

for any
s\in\mathbb{{R}}
and
v
in
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{{s}}}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}(\operatorname{div})
with zero divergence.

Proof.

Since
\operatorname{div}v=0
, by (
43c
),
u={{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}v
is in

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{{s}}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
and satisfies
\operatorname{curl}u=v
in all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
. For any
constant vector
b\in\mathbb{{V}}
, the action of the distribution
u
on
b
satisfies

\displaystyle 2\,u(b)=u(\operatorname{curl}(b\times x))=(\operatorname{curl}u)(b\times x)=v(b\times x)=0

since
v(r)=0
for any
r\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}
. Hence, applying
{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}
to each
component of
u
and using (
43d
), we obtain a

\tau\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{{s+2}}}\otimes\mathbb{{M}}
such that
\operatorname{div}\tau=u
, i.e.,

\displaystyle v=\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau=\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\operatorname{dev}\tau,

since
\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}(\frac{1}{3}(\operatorname{tr}\tau){\mathbbm{i}})=0.
Denoting the map

v\mapsto\operatorname{dev}\tau
by
{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cd}}}}
, the proof is finished by the
continuity of
{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}
and
{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}
.
∎

Theorem 3.10
.

There exist four continuous linear operators

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}},\quad\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\quad\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\quad\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({3})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\quad

such that any
\tau\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}
can be decomposed into

\tau=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau+\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau+\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({3})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau.

(57)

It then follows that
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}=\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
.

Proof.

Let
\tau\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}
and put
q=\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau
. Obviously

\operatorname{div}q=0
and
q\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div})
. Moreover, for any

a,b\in\mathbb{{V}}
and
r=a+b\times x\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}
, since

\operatorname{curl}r=2b
is constant, its gradient vanishes, and

\displaystyle q(r)=(\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau)(r)=\tau(\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{curl}r)=0.

Thus
q
is in
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div})
and we apply
\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cd}}}
to it. Put

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau:={\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cd}}}}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau
. By
Lemma
3.9
with
s=-1
, we find that

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
and

\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}(\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)=0.

Hence we may apply Lemma
3.8
to get

\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau=(\operatorname{curl}{\mathop{\hat{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}+\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}\mathop{\hat{U}_{\mathrm{gc}}}+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\hat{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}})(\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau).

The decomposition (
57
) now follows after setting

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau={\mathop{\hat{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}(\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)
,

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau=\mathop{\hat{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}(\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)
and

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({3})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau=\mathop{\hat{U}_{\mathrm{gc}}}(\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)
.

To prove that
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}=\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
, let
\tau\in\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}
be decomposed as in (
57
).
By the density of
\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)
in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}
,
there are

\tau_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
,

g_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
,

q_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
, and

u_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
,
such that

\|\tau_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\quad\|g_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\quad\|q_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\quad\|u_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,

as
m\to\infty.

By (
31
),

\displaystyle\|\tau_{m}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{curl}g_{m}+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}q_{m}+\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}u_{m}\;-\;\tau\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}

\displaystyle=\|\tau_{m}-\tau\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}+\|\operatorname{curl}(g_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)\|_{L_{2}}+\|\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}(q_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle+\|\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}(u_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({3})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)\|_{L_{2}}\to 0,

which converges to zero as
m\to\infty
in view of
(
18
). Thus
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}\subseteq\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
and the proof is
complete due to (
42
).
∎

In view of these results, we shall no longer distinguish between

\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
,
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
, nor
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
.

3.4.
Continuous right inverses

Let us now complete the discussion of (
41
).
Several right inverse operators in (
41
) were
already given in previous lemmas. The right inverses in the top row
of (
41
) are the same operators as
in (
43b
)–(
43d
). For example,
{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}

is a right inverse of
\operatorname{curl}:{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})\to{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})
in the sense
that
{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}:{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div}))\to{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
is continuous and

\operatorname{curl}\circ{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}
equals the identity on
\ker(\operatorname{div}:{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div}))
,
which is just a restatement of (
43c
) with
s=0
.
After construction of the remaining needed right inverses,
Theorem
3.21
below gathers everything together.

Lemma 3.11
.

There are linear maps

{\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})\to L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}\subset\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
and

\mathop{\tilde{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\subset{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
such that for all

\tau\in\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})
,

\tau=\operatorname{curl}({\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\tilde{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau),\qquad\|{\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau\|_{L_{2}}+\|\mathop{\tilde{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}.

Proof.

Applying
{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}
to the divergence-free row vectors of
\tau
, we find a

\gamma\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{M}}
satisfying
\operatorname{curl}\gamma=\tau
per
(
43c
). Put
g=\operatorname{sym}\gamma
and
v=\operatorname{vskw}\gamma
. Then,
by (
26e
),

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}(\operatorname{sym}\gamma+\operatorname{skw}\gamma)=\operatorname{curl}g-\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{mskw}v

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}g-S\operatorname{grad}v

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}g-\T\operatorname{grad}v+(\operatorname{div}v){\mathbbm{i}}.

Since
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
consists of trace-free matrix fields and since trace of

\operatorname{curl}g
vanishes by (
26f
), taking the trace of the
above expression, we find that

\displaystyle 0=\operatorname{tr}\tau=2\operatorname{div}v.

Therefore, by (
43c
),

u=-\frac{1}{2}{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}v\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},
satisfies

v=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}u
, so

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}g+\frac{1}{2}\T\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{curl}u

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}(g+\operatorname{def}u)

by (
31
) of Lemma
2.1
. The linear
maps
\tau\mapsto g
and
\tau\mapsto u
are the needed
{\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}
and

\mathop{\tilde{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.12
.

There is a linear map

{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gc}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})\to L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}\subset\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
such that for all

\tau\in\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})
,

\tau=\operatorname{curl}{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau,\qquad\|{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}.

Proof.

Using the operators of Lemma
3.11
, define

{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau={\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\tilde{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau
. Then the result follows
immediately from Lemmas
3.11
and (
18
).
∎

Lemma 3.13
.

There is a linear map

\mathop{D_{\mathrm{\mathrm{gc}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})\cap\ker(\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\subset{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
such that for all
\tau\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}

with
\operatorname{div}\tau=0
and
\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau=0
,

\tau=\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}\mathop{D_{\mathrm{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau,\qquad\|\mathop{D_{\mathrm{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}

Proof.

Given any
\tau\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
with
\operatorname{div}\tau=0
, by
Lemma
3.11
,

\tau=\operatorname{curl}({\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\tilde{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau)
. When

\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau
also vanishes, this implies that

0=\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau=\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\operatorname{curl}({\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\tilde{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau)=\operatorname{inc}({\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau).

Applying Lemma
3.2
with
g={\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau
,

\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}g=\operatorname{curl}g,

which in turn implies that

\tau=\operatorname{curl}({\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\tilde{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau)=\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}({\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}{\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}\tau+\mathop{\tilde{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}\tau).

Hence the result follows by setting
\mathop{D_{\mathrm{\mathrm{gc}}}}={\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}{\mathop{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}+\mathop{\tilde{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.14
.

There is a linear map

{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gd}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{curl}:{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl}))\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
such
that for all
v\in\ker(\operatorname{curl}:{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl}))

v=\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{div}{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gd}}}}v,\qquad\|{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gd}}}}v\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|v\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}.

(58)

Proof.

Let
v\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl})
have zero curl. Then
w={{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}v
is in

L_{2}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
, supported on
\bar{\varOmega}
, and satisfies

v=\operatorname{grad}w
in all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
due to (
43b
). Since

v(r)=0
for all
r\in\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}
, choosing
r=bx
for any constant
b
,

0=(\operatorname{grad}w)(r)=(w,\operatorname{div}r)=3(w,b).

Hence we may apply
{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}
to each component of
w
and
use (
43d
) to get a
q\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}

satisfying
w=\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{div}q
. The linear map
v\mapsto w

is the required
{\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gd}}}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.15
.

There is a linear map
{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gg}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{inc}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}})\to{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
such
that for any
g\in\ker(\operatorname{inc}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}})
,

\operatorname{def}{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gg}}}}g=g,\qquad\|{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gg}}}}g\|_{H(\operatorname{curl})}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.2
,
u={\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}g
satisfies
\operatorname{curl}(g-\operatorname{def}u)=0
.
Hence applying
{{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}
to each row
vector of
g-\operatorname{def}u
, we obtain a
v\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
such
that
g-\operatorname{def}u=\operatorname{grad}v
. The symmetry of the left hand side implies that

0=\operatorname{skw}(g-\operatorname{def}u)=\operatorname{skw}\operatorname{grad}v=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{mskw}\operatorname{curl}v

by (
26c
). Hence
\operatorname{curl}v=0
on all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
, so
the vector field
v|_{\varOmega}
is in
{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
. We have thus shown
that
g=\operatorname{def}(u+v)
. Letting the map
g\mapsto u+v
be
denoted by
{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gg}}}}
, the norm bound follows from the continuity of

{{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}
and
{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{gg}}}}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.16
.

There is a linear map

{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gd}}}}:{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl})\to L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}\subset\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}

such that for
all
v\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl})
,

v=\operatorname{div}{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gd}}}}v,\qquad\|{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gd}}}}v\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|v\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}.

(59)

Proof.

Let
v\in\mathcal{{D}}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}
. Since every component
v_{i}
of
v
has
zero mean on
\varOmega
, we may apply
{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}
to each and use (
43d
)
to get a
\tau\in L_{2}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})\otimes\mathbb{{M}}
, supported on

\bar{\varOmega}
, satisfying
v=\operatorname{div}\tau
on all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
, which
in particular, implies
that
\tau
is in
{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})
. Hence we may integrate by parts to conclude that

\displaystyle(\tau,\operatorname{grad}r)=(\operatorname{div}\tau,r)=(v,r)=0

for all
r\in\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}
. Choosing
r=bx
for any

b\in\mathbb{{R}}
and noting that
\operatorname{grad}(bx)=b{\mathbbm{i}}
, we find that

(\tau,{\mathbbm{i}})=0
. Hence
t=\operatorname{tr}(\tau)\in L_{2}
satisfies

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=(\tau,{\mathbbm{i}})=(\operatorname{dev}\tau,{\mathbbm{i}})+\frac{1}{3}(t{\mathbbm{i}},{\mathbbm{i}})=(t,1).

Now, by (
43d
),
q={{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}t
satisfies
\operatorname{div}q=t
, so

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}\tau=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{dev}\tau+\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{div}(\operatorname{tr}\tau)\,{\mathbbm{i}}=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{dev}\tau+\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}(t)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}(\operatorname{dev}\tau)+\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}(\operatorname{div}q)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}(\operatorname{dev}\tau)+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{div}(\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}q)

by (
32
) of Lemma
2.1
. Denoting the
linear maps
v\mapsto\operatorname{dev}\tau+\frac{1}{2}\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}q
by
{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gd}}}}
,
the norm estimate in (
59
)
follows for
v\in\mathcal{{D}}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}
. The proof is now finished by
the density result of Lemma
2.2
.
∎

Lemma 3.17
.

There is a linear map
{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gc}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}})\to{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})

such that for all
\tau\in\ker(\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}})
,

\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gc}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}}}\tau=\tau,\qquad\|{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gc}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}}}\tau\|_{H(\operatorname{div})}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}.

Proof.

Let
\tau\in\ker(\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}})
. Then

\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\T\tau=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\tau=0
, so
by (
43b
),
w={{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}\operatorname{div}(\T\tau)
in

L_{2}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})
, supported on
\bar{\varOmega}
, satisfies

\operatorname{grad}w=\operatorname{div}\T\tau
.

Next, recalling that
\operatorname{tr}\tau=0
, note that

\displaystyle 2\operatorname{skw}\operatorname{curl}\tau

\displaystyle=\operatorname{mskw}\operatorname{div}S\tau

by (
26d
)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{mskw}\operatorname{div}\T\tau=\operatorname{mskw}\operatorname{grad}w

\displaystyle=-\operatorname{curl}(w{\mathbbm{i}}),

\displaystyle\text{ by~\eqref{eq:mskw-grad}}.

Hence

\operatorname{curl}\tau=\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\tau+\operatorname{skw}\operatorname{curl}\tau=-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}(w{\mathbbm{i}}).
Applying
{{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}
to each row vector of

\tau+\frac{1}{2}w{\mathbbm{i}}
, (
43b
) we obtain a

q\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
satisfying

\tau+\frac{1}{2}w{\mathbbm{i}}=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{grad}q.

(60)

In particular, applying the
\operatorname{tr}
-operator to both sides
of (
60
), we see that the identity
3w=\operatorname{div}q
holds on
all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
, so
q\in{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})
. Furthermore, applying

\operatorname{dev}
-operator to both sides of (
60
), we conclude that

\tau=\operatorname{dev}\tau=\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}q
. The map
\tau\mapsto q
is the
required operator
{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gc}}}}
and its stated norm bound follows from the
continuity of
{{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.18
.

There is a linear map

{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{cc}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}})\to L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}

such that for all

\sigma\in\ker(\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}})
,

\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{cc}}}}\sigma=\sigma,\qquad\|{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{cc}}}}\sigma\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|\sigma\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.6
,
\sigma-\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}\sigma
has
vanishing divergence. Applying
{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}
to its rows and
using (
43c
), we obtain a

\rho\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{M}}
such that

\sigma-\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}\sigma=\operatorname{curl}\rho
. Hence

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}\sigma+\operatorname{curl}(\operatorname{dev}\rho+\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{tr}(\rho){\mathbbm{i}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}\sigma+\operatorname{curl}(\operatorname{dev}\rho)-\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{mskw}\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{tr}(\rho)

by (
26b
). Applying
\operatorname{sym}
-operator to both sides,

\sigma=\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}({\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}\sigma+\operatorname{dev}\rho)
. The linear map

\sigma\mapsto{\mathop{{\tilde{R}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{cc}}}}}}\sigma+\operatorname{dev}\rho
is the required map

{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{cc}}}}
.
∎

Lemma 3.19
.

There is a linear map

{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{cd}}}}:{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div})\to L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}

such that for all

q
in
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div})
,

q=\operatorname{div}{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{cd}}}}q,\qquad\|{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{cd}}}}q\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|q\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div})}.

(61)

Proof.

Let
q\in\mathcal{{D}}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}
. Since every component of
q
has zero
mean, by (
43d
), there exists a

\gamma\in L_{2}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})\otimes\mathbb{{M}}
, supported on
\bar{\varOmega}
,
such that
\operatorname{div}\gamma=q
. In particular, this implies that each
row vector of
\gamma|_{\varOmega}
is in
{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})
. Hence, integration by
parts shows that for any
b\in\mathbb{{V}}
,

\displaystyle(q,b\times x)=(\operatorname{div}\gamma,b\times x)=-(\gamma,\operatorname{grad}(b\times x))=-(\gamma,\operatorname{mskw}b).

Since
(q,b\times x)=0
for all
q\in\mathcal{{D}}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}
, all terms
above vanish, so
0=(\gamma,\operatorname{mskw}b)=(\operatorname{mskw}u,\operatorname{mskw}b)
with

u=\operatorname{vskw}\gamma,
or equivalently,
(u,b)=0
. Therefore,
applying (
43d
) to each component of

u\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
, we obtain a

\tau\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{M}}
such that
\operatorname{div}\tau=-2u
.

Collecting these observations, and putting
\sigma=\operatorname{sym}\gamma
,

\displaystyle q=\operatorname{div}\gamma

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}(\operatorname{sym}\gamma+\operatorname{mskw}u)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}\sigma-\operatorname{curl}u

\displaystyle\text{by~\eqref{eq:div-mskw}},

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\operatorname{dev}\tau

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}(\sigma+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\operatorname{dev}\tau)

\displaystyle\text{by~\eqref{eq:4}}.

Set
{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{cd}}}}q=\sigma+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\operatorname{dev}\tau
. Then by the
continuity of
{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}
in (
43d
), the norm estimate in
(
61
) follows for any
q\in\mathcal{{D}}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}
. The proof is
finished using the density result of Lemma
2.2
.
∎

Lemma 3.20
.

There are continuous linear maps

{{R}_{\mathrm{g}}}:\ker(\operatorname{curl}:{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl}))\to L_{2,\mathbb{R}}
,

{{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}:{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div}))\to(L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}})\cap{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl})
,
and

{{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}:{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}}\to(L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}})\cap{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div})
,
such that for any
v\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl})
with
\operatorname{curl}v=0
,

q\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div})
with
\operatorname{div}q=0
, and
w\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}{{R}_{\mathrm{g}}}v=v,
