\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}{{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q=q,

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}{{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w=w,

\displaystyle\|{{R}_{\mathrm{g}}}v\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|v\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}

\displaystyle\|{{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|q\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}},

\displaystyle\|{{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|w\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}.

Proof.

Let us construct the last operator first. The functional action of
any
w\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}}
on constant functions vanish, so we
use (
43d
) to conclude that

{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
satisfies
\operatorname{div}{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w=w
.

We proceed to correct
{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}
to obtain orthogonality to
\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}
.
Let
u\in{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
satisfy

\displaystyle(\operatorname{curl}u,\operatorname{curl}v)=({{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w,\operatorname{curl}v),\qquad\operatorname{div}u=0,

for all
v\in{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
, a constrained formulation that is well
known to be uniquely solvable
[
31
]
. The first equation
above implies that

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{curl}u\|_{L_{2}}\leq\|{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|w\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}},

(62)

\displaystyle\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{curl}u=\operatorname{curl}{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w.

(63)

Put
{{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w:={{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w-\operatorname{curl}u\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
. It satisfies

\operatorname{curl}{{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w=0,\qquad\operatorname{div}{{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w=w,\qquad({{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w,r)=0

for all
r\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}
. Indeed, the first equation follows
from (
63
) and the second from
\operatorname{div}{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w=w
. To see the
third, first note that since the functional action of
w
on any

p_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
vanish,

0=w(p_{1})=(\operatorname{div}{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w)(p_{1})=({{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w,\operatorname{grad}p_{1}),

so

({{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}w,a)=0
for any
a\in\mathbb{{V}}.
Combined with

(\operatorname{curl}u,a)=(u,\operatorname{curl}a)=0
, we have
({{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w,a)=0
.
Moreover, since
\operatorname{curl}(bx\cdot x)=-2b\times x
for any

b\in\mathbb{{V}}
, we have

\displaystyle 0=(\operatorname{curl}{{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w)(bx\cdot x)=({{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w,\operatorname{curl}(bx\cdot x))=({{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w,-2b\times x),

so
{{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w
is
L_{2}
-orthogonal to
a+b\times x
for any
a,b\in\mathbb{{V}}
.
By the continuity of
{{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}
and (
62
), we also obtain
the norm bound
\|{{R}_{\mathrm{d}}}w\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\|w\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}
.

The construction of
{{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}
is similar: set

{{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q:=2({{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}q-\operatorname{grad}w)
where
w\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}
solves

(\operatorname{grad}w,\operatorname{grad}z)=({{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}q,\operatorname{grad}z)
for all
z\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}
. Clearly,
\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}{{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q=q
. Also, for any

a\in\mathbb{{V}}
, since the action of
q
on any
\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}
-function
vanishes,

0=q(a\times x)=(\operatorname{curl}{{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}q)(a\times x)=({{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}q,2a)
, so

({{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q,a)=0
. Moreover, for any
b\in\mathbb{{R}}
,

({{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q,a+bx)=({{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q,bx)=\frac{1}{2}({{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q,\operatorname{grad}(bx\cdot x))=\frac{1}{2}(\operatorname{div}{{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q)(b|x|^{2})

which must vanish since
\operatorname{div}{{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q=0
by construction, so
{{R}_{\mathrm{c}}}q

is
L_{2}
-orthogonal to
\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}
.

Finally, simply setting
{{R}_{\mathrm{g}}}=3{{T}_{\mathrm{g}}}
, it is easy to verify the
stated properties of
{{R}_{\mathrm{g}}}
.
∎

Theorem 3.21
.

Each
R
and
D
operator in (
41
) is a
continuous right inverse of the differential operator marked above
it.

Proof.

The result is proved by prior lemmas, as seen by the
following pointers, which consider the operators, row by row, from
left to right, but omitting the obvious ones in the first row and
any obvious symmetrically opposite ones.

•

\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gg}}}:\ker(\operatorname{curl}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}})\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}
is a continuous right
inverse of
\operatorname{hess}:{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{grad})\to\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
by Lemma
3.3

applied with
s=-1
.

\mathop{D_{\mathrm{\mathrm{gc}}}}:\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})\cap\ker(\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\subset{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
is a continuous right inverse of

\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}:{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})\to\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}

3.13
.

is a continuous right inverse of

is a continuous
right inverse of
\operatorname{def}:{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})\to\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}

Corollary 3.22
.

The range of every differential operator
in (
41
) is closed.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of the existence of continuous
right inverses for each differential operator
in (
41
), as we have shown.

For example, consider the operator
\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}.
Clearly its range
is contained in both
\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})
and

\ker(\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})
. But Lemma
3.13
shows that
the intersection of these kernels is also contained in the range of

\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}
, so

\operatorname{range}(\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def})=\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}})\cap\ker(\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}).

By the continuity of the differential operators proved in
Theorem
2.3
, both the above kernels are
closed, and so is their intersection. Hence the range of

\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}
is closed.

The proofs for the remaining operators are similar and simpler.
∎

Corollary 3.23
.

The hessian complex (
38
), the elasticity
complex (
39
), and the div-div complex
(
40
) are exact complexes.

Proof.

To prove that the hessian complex (
38
) is
exact it suffices to prove that

\operatorname{range}(\operatorname{hess})\supseteq\ker(\operatorname{curl}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}),

\operatorname{range}(\operatorname{curl})\supseteq\ker(\operatorname{div}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}),

and

\operatorname{range}(\operatorname{div})\supseteq{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl})

(since the reverse inclusions are clear from
Corollary
2.5
). But these are now obvious by the
existence of continuous right inverses
\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gg}}},{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gc}}}},
and
{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gd}}}}

given by Lemmas
3.3
,
3.12
, and
3.14
,
respectively.

Similarly, the continuous right inverse operators
\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gg}}}
,
\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cc}}}
,
and
{\mathop{R_{\mathrm{cd}}}}
, given by Lemmas
3.15
,
3.1
,
and
3.19
, prove the exactness of the elasticity complex.

The exactness of the div div complex similarly follows from the
continuous right inverse operators of
\mathop{R_{\mathrm{gc}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}},\mathop{R_{\mathrm{cc}}}
and
\mathop{D_{\mathrm{dd}}}
of
Lemmas
3.17
,
3.18
, and
3.5
.
∎

4.
Slightly more regular spaces of matrix fields

Consider the following slightly more regular spaces of matrix fields,
contained in the previously introduced spaces
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
,
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
and

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
:

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}

\displaystyle=\{g\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}:\operatorname{inc}g\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}\},

(64)

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}

\displaystyle=\{\tau\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}:\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\},

(65)

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}

\displaystyle=\{\sigma\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}:\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\},

(66)

whose natural norms are defined respectively by

\displaystyle\|g\|^{2}_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}}

\displaystyle=\|g\|_{L_{2}}^{2}+\|\operatorname{inc}g\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}^{2},

\displaystyle\|\tau\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\tau\|_{L_{2}}^{2}+\|\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}^{2},

\displaystyle\|\sigma\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\sigma\|_{L_{2}}^{2}+\|\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}^{2}.

Such spaces of matrix fields and their even smoother versions, have
emerged in recent works
[
2
,
20
,
38
]
.

Note that one possible way to increase the regularity of the prior
spaces
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
,
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
is to uniformly replace
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}
by

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{{s}}}
with some
s>-1
in (
23
) (and we
expect the prior analysis to go through with minimal changes for such
modification). The new spaces of this section,
\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}},\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}},
and

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
, are not obtained this way. Instead, they are obtained by increasing
the regularity of the matrix-valued function to
L_{2}
while
maintaining the same
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}
-regularity for its second-order derivative.
We proceed to prove regular decompositions and density of smooth
functions for such spaces.

Theorem 4.1

(Regular decomposition of
\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
)
.

There exist continuous linear operators

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}:\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
,

\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}:\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}

such that any
g\in\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}

can be decomposed into

g=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g.

(67)

Consequently,

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}=\overline{\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}}^{\|\cdot\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}}}.

(68)

Proof.

Since any
g\in\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
in also in
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
, we apply the operators

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}
and
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}
of Theorem
3.4
and follow
along the lines of its proof to obtain (
46
),

\operatorname{curl}\big(g-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g-\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g\big)=0.
Setting

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g=\mathop{D_{\mathrm{gg}}}(g-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g-\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g)
we apply
Lemma
3.3
, but this time with
s=0
since
g
is now in

L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
, to get that

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{2}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\qquad g-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g-\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g=\operatorname{hess}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g.

Now (
67
) follows setting

\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g
.

To prove (
68
), decompose
g\in\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
as
above. By the density of
\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)
in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}
, there are

g_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
and

u_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
such that

\|g_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\qquad\|u_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\qquad

as
m\to\infty
. Hence,
g_{m}+\operatorname{def}u_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
and

\displaystyle\|g-(g_{m}+\operatorname{def}u_{m})\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}}^{2}\leq\|g-g_{m}\|_{L_{2}}^{2}+\|\operatorname{inc}(g-g_{m})\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}^{2}\lesssim\|g-g_{m}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

by (
18
). Since the last term converges to zero, we
have proved that
\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
is contained in the closure of

\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
. The reverse inclusion is obvious.
∎

Theorem 4.2

(Regular decomposition of
\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
)
.

There exist continuous linear operators

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
and

\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}:\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}

such that any
\sigma\in\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}

can be decomposed into

\sigma=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma.

(69)

Consequently,

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}=\overline{\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}}^{\|\cdot\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}}}.

(70)

Proof.

We proceed along the lines of the proof of
Theorem
3.7
, but now with the more regular

\sigma
in
\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
, to obtain that

\operatorname{div}\big(\sigma-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma-\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma\big)=0,

with
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}
and
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}
as defined there. At this point, we apply
Lemma
3.1
, now with
s=0
,
setting

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma:={\mathop{D_{\mathrm{cc}}}}(\sigma-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma-\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma)
, to find that

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}\sigma\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{{2}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\qquad\sigma-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma-\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma=\operatorname{inc}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma.

The result now follows setting

\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma+\T\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma
.

To conclude, let
\sigma\in\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
and use the just proved
decomposition (
69
) to split it into

\sigma=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma.
By the density
of
\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)
in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}
, there are

\sigma_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
and

\tau_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
such that

\|\sigma_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\qquad\|\tau_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\qquad

as
m\to\infty
. Hence, by (
18
) and (
30
)

\sigma_{m}+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\tau_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
converges
to
\sigma
in
\|\cdot\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}}
norm, thus proving that

\sigma
is contained in the closure of
\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
. Since the reverse inclusion is obvious,
(
70
) is proved.
∎

Theorem 4.3

(Regular decomposition of
\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
)
.

There exist three continuous linear operators

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}},\quad\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\quad\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},

such that any
\tau\in\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
can be decomposed into

\tau=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau+\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau.

(71)

Consequently,

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}=\overline{\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{T}}}^{\|\cdot\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}}.

(72)

Proof.

We follow along the lines of the proof of
Theorem
3.10
to find that given any

\tau\in\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
, using the same
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}
operator defined there, we
have
\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}(\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)=0
. But now, since the higher
regularity of
\tau
implies that
\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau
is in

L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
, we may apply the two-term decomposition of
Lemma
3.8
(taking
\mathop{\hat{U}_{\mathrm{gc}}}
there to be zero) instead
of the three-term decomposition to obtain

\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau=(\operatorname{curl}{\mathop{\hat{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\hat{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}})(\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau).

The decomposition (
71
) now follows after
setting
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau={\mathop{\hat{R}_{\mathrm{gc}}}}(\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)
, and

\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau=\mathop{\hat{D}_{\mathrm{gc}}}(\tau-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)
.

To prove (
72
), let
\tau\in\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
be
decomposed as in (
71
). By the density of

\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)
in
{\mathring{H}}^{1}
, there are

\tau_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
,

g_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
and

q_{m}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
which converge to the
decomposition components
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau
,
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau
and

\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau
, respectively, in the
H^{1}
-norm, as

m\to\infty
. Hence

\displaystyle\|\tau_{m}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{curl}g_{m}+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}q_{m}-\tau\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\tau_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}+\|\operatorname{curl}(g_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}+\|\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}(q_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)\|_{\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}+\|\operatorname{curl}(g_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)\|_{L_{2}}+\|\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}(q_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau)\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}+\|g_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}+\|q_{m}-\mathop{\mathring{\mathcal{S}}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau\|_{H^{1}}

where we have used (
31
) (which implies that

\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\circ\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}=0
) and (
18
). Since
the last bound converges to zero as
m\to\infty
, we have just
exhibited a sequence in
\mathcal{{\mathcal{D}}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
that
approximates any given
\tau\in\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
arbitrarily closely in

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{\!\mathcal{H}}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
norm.
∎

5.
Duality

In this section, we state extensions to 2-complexes built
using Sobolev spaces that are generally not closures of compactly
supported smooth functions, such as those
in (
14
), as well as
spaces built using
{{H}}^{-1}
instead of
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}
, such as

\displaystyle{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{curl})

\displaystyle=\{u\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}:\operatorname{curl}u\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\},

\displaystyle{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})

\displaystyle=\{q\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}:\operatorname{div}q\in{{H}}^{-1}\},

spaces of matrix-valued functions,

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{cc}}

\displaystyle=\{g\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}:\operatorname{curl}g\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\operatorname{inc}g\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}\},

(73a)

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{cd}}

\displaystyle=\{\tau\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}:\operatorname{div}\tau\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\tau\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\},

(73b)

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{dd}}

\displaystyle=\{\sigma\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}:\operatorname{div}\sigma\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma\in{{H}}^{-1}\},

(73c)

and their slightly more regular versions,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}

\displaystyle=\{g\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}:\operatorname{inc}g\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}\},

(74a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}

\displaystyle=\{\sigma\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}:\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma\in{{H}}^{-1}\},

(74b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}

\displaystyle=\{\tau\in L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}:\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau\in{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\}.

(74c)

Analogues of previous results can be proved for

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{curl})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{div})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{curl})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathrm{cc}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathrm{cd}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{-1}(\operatorname{curl})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{div})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathrm{dd}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{-1}(\operatorname{div})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{-1}(\operatorname{curl})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{-1}(\operatorname{div})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{hess}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{def}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\;\;\frac{1}{2}\!\!\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{def}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\T\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\T\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}\T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}

(75)

using exactly the same techniques. They are summarized in the next
theorem. We use
\mathcal{{D}}(\bar{\varOmega})
to denote the space of restrictions of functions
in
\mathcal{{D}}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})
to the closure
\bar{\varOmega}
.

Theorem 5.1
.

(1)

The diagram (
75
) commutes.

(2)

Every path in it is a 2-complex.

(3)

Every differential operator in it is continuous, has closed
range, and has a continuous right inverse.

(4)

There are regular decompositions for
H_{\mathrm{cc}},H_{\mathrm{dd}},
and
H_{\mathrm{cd}}
. Namely,
there exist continuous linear operators

\mathop{S^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}:H_{\mathrm{cc}}\to{H}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\;\mathop{S^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}:H_{\mathrm{cc}}\to{H}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\;\mathop{S^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}:H_{\mathrm{cc}}\to{H}^{1},\mathop{S^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}:H_{\mathrm{dd}}\to{H}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\;\mathop{S^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}:H_{\mathrm{dd}}\to{H}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}},\;\mathop{S^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}:H_{\mathrm{dd}}\to{H}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\mathop{S^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:H_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{H}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}},\;\mathop{S^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:H_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{H}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\;\mathop{S^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:H_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{H}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\;\mathop{S^{({3})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:H_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{H}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},\;

such that

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=\mathop{S^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{def}\mathop{S^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{hess}\mathop{S^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g,

\displaystyle g\in H_{\mathrm{cc}},

(76)

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=\mathop{S^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{S^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma+\operatorname{inc}\mathop{S^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma,

\displaystyle\sigma\in H_{\mathrm{dd}},

(77)

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\mathop{S^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau+\operatorname{curl}\mathop{S^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathop{S^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau+\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}\mathop{S^{({3})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau,

\displaystyle\tau\in H_{\mathrm{cd}}.

(78)

(5)

The spaces

\mathcal{{D}}(\bar{\varOmega})\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\mathcal{{D}}(\bar{\varOmega})\otimes\mathbb{{T}},

and
\mathcal{{D}}(\bar{\varOmega})\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
are dense in
H_{\mathrm{cc}},H_{\mathrm{cd}},

and
H_{\mathrm{dd}}
, respectively.

(6)

There are regular decompositions for
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cc}},\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dd}},
and

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}
. Namely, there are continuous linear operators

\mathop{\mathcal{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cc}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
,

\mathop{\mathcal{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
,

\mathop{\mathcal{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}\to{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
,
such that

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=\mathop{S^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathcal{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g,

\displaystyle g\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cc}},

(79)

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle=\mathop{S^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau+\operatorname{curl}\mathop{S^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\mathcal{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\tau,

\displaystyle\tau\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cd}},

(80)

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=\mathop{S^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathcal{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma,

\displaystyle\sigma\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}.

(81)

(7)

The spaces

\mathcal{{D}}(\bar{\varOmega})\otimes\mathbb{{S}},\mathcal{{D}}(\bar{\varOmega})\otimes\mathbb{{T}},

and
\mathcal{{D}}(\bar{\varOmega})\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
are dense in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cc}},\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{cd}},

and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dd}}
, respectively.

Proof.

Proceed as in the proofs of Theorems
2.3
,

2.4
, and
3.21
.
∎

Some spaces at the edges of the diagram (
75
) can
be restricted to certain subspaces of interest without affecting the
matrix-valued function spaces to get the following commuting diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}/\mathcal{P}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{curl})/\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{div})/\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2}/\mathbb{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{curl})/\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathrm{cc}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathrm{cd}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{-1}(\operatorname{curl})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{div})/\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathrm{dd}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{-1}(\operatorname{div})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2}/\mathbb{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{-1}(\operatorname{curl})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{-1}(\operatorname{div})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{hess}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{def}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\;\;\frac{1}{2}\!\!\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{def}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\T\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\T\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}\T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{3}\operatorname{grad}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{div}}

(82)

This is because of the following facts:
(a)
\operatorname{grad}\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathbb{{V}}
is in the zero coset of
H(\operatorname{curl})/\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}
,
(b)
\operatorname{curl}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}=\mathbb{{V}}
is in the zero coset of
H(\operatorname{div})/\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}
,
(c)
\operatorname{div}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}=\mathbb{{R}}
is in the zero coset of
L_{2}/\mathbb{{R}}
,
(d)
\operatorname{def}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}=0
, and
(e)
\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}=0
.

Next we turn to establishing certain duality relationships between the spaces in the diagram (
25
) and those in the just introduced diagram (
82
).
First
we need a lemma that enlarges the domain of
certain functionals. Consider a
q\in H^{-1}(\operatorname{div})
. Then, since
q

is in
H^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
, its action on a function in

{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
is well defined, so the action of
q
on
gradient fields,
q(\operatorname{grad}w)
, is well defined provided

\operatorname{grad}w\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
. But in fact, it is also well defined if

\operatorname{grad}w
is just in
L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
, as stated next, where similar
other extensions are also collected.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
q\in H^{-1}(\operatorname{div})
,
\sigma\in H_{\mathrm{dd}}
,
g\in H_{\mathrm{cc}}
, and

\tau\in H_{\mathrm{cd}}
. Then
q\circ\operatorname{grad}
,
\sigma\circ\operatorname{def}
, extends
to continuous linear maps such that

\displaystyle q\circ\operatorname{grad}

\displaystyle:{\mathring{H}}^{1}\to\mathbb{{R}},

\displaystyle\quad(q\circ\operatorname{grad})(w)

\displaystyle=-(\operatorname{div}q)(w),

\displaystyle\quad w\in{\mathring{H}}^{1},

(83a)

\displaystyle\sigma\circ\operatorname{def}

\displaystyle:{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\to\mathbb{{R}},

\displaystyle\quad(\sigma\circ\operatorname{def})(u)

\displaystyle=-(\operatorname{div}\sigma)(u),

\displaystyle\quad u\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},

(83b)

\displaystyle\sigma\circ\operatorname{hess}

\displaystyle:{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}\to\mathbb{{R}},

\displaystyle\quad(\sigma\circ\operatorname{hess})(w)

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma)(w),

\displaystyle\quad w\in{\mathring{H}}^{1},

(83c)

\displaystyle g\circ\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}

\displaystyle:{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}\to\mathbb{{R}},

\displaystyle\quad(g\circ\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl})(\eta)

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{curl}g)(\eta),

\displaystyle\quad\eta\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}},

(83d)

\displaystyle g\circ\operatorname{inc}

\displaystyle:{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}\to\mathbb{{R}},

\displaystyle\quad(g\circ\operatorname{inc})(\gamma)

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{inc}g)(\gamma),

\displaystyle\quad\gamma\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},

(83e)

\displaystyle\tau\circ\operatorname{curl}

\displaystyle:{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}\to\mathbb{{R}},

\displaystyle\quad(\tau\circ\operatorname{curl})(\gamma)

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\tau)(\gamma),

\displaystyle\quad\gamma\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}},

(83f)

\displaystyle\tau\circ\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}

\displaystyle:{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\to\mathbb{{R}},

\displaystyle\quad(\tau\circ\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad})(u)

\displaystyle=-(\operatorname{div}\tau)(u),

\displaystyle\quad u\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},

(83g)

\displaystyle\tau\circ\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}

\displaystyle:{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}\to\mathbb{{R}},

\displaystyle\quad(\tau\circ\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def})(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau)(u),

\displaystyle\quad u\in{\mathring{H}}^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}},

(83h)

where, component wise, the right hand sides are all duality pairings
between
{{H}}^{-1}
and
{\mathring{H}}^{1}
.

Proof.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)
, by the definition of the distributional
divergence,

(\operatorname{div}q)(\varphi)=-q(\operatorname{grad}\varphi)\equiv-(q\circ\operatorname{grad})(\varphi).
Since
\operatorname{div}q
is in
{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})
,

|(q\circ\operatorname{grad})(\varphi)|\leq\|\operatorname{div}q\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}\|\varphi\|_{{\mathring{H}}^{1}}

showing that
q\circ\operatorname{grad}
can be continuously extended to the
closure of
\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)
in the
{\mathring{H}}^{1}
-norm, i.e., to
{\mathring{H}}^{1}
. This
proves the first statement. The remaining statements are proved
similarly, noting that at times we must use the identities of
Lemma
2.1
: e.g., for any given

\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
, using (
28
) and (
31
),
we have

(\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau)(\varphi)=(\operatorname{div}\T\operatorname{curl}\T\tau)(\varphi)=\T\tau(\operatorname{curl}\T\operatorname{grad}\varphi)=\T\tau(\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{curl}\varphi)=2\tau(\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}\varphi).

∎

It was proved in
[
21
]
that the dual of

{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})
equals
{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{curl})
, both algebraically and topologically.
The next lemma states this together with closely related identities.

Lemma 5.3
.

The following equalities of spaces hold algebraically and topologically.

\displaystyle{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{-1}(\operatorname{curl}),

\displaystyle{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{-1}(\operatorname{div}),

\displaystyle H(\operatorname{div})^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{curl}),

\displaystyle H(\operatorname{curl})^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div}).

Proof.

The first identity was proved in

[
21
, Theorem 2.2]
. Here we prove the second
and the last (since the third is similar). The proofs are
presented step by step below, but a unified strategy for proving all identities
will be evident: we use the Riesz representative of a functional in

X^{*}
to show that
X^{*}\hookrightarrow Y
, and then use a regular
decomposition of
X
to prove that
Y\hookrightarrow X^{*}
.

Step 1
.
{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}\hookrightarrow{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})
:
To prove
that
{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}
is continuously embedded in
{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})
,
consider an
f\in{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}
.
By the Riesz representation theorem, there is a
u_{f}\in{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})

such that

f(v)=(u_{f},v)+(\operatorname{curl}u_{f},\operatorname{curl}v),\qquad v\in{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl}).

Choosing
v\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
we find that the equality

f=u_{f}+\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{curl}u_{f}

holds as distributions on
\varOmega
. It immediately follows that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|u_{f}+\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{curl}u_{f}\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}^{2}+\|\operatorname{div}u_{f}\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{f}\|_{{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})}=\|f\|_{{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}}.

This proves that the restriction of the distribution
f
to

\bar{\varOmega}
is an element of
{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})
and that the embedding of

{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}
into
{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})
is continuous.

Step 2
.
{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})\hookrightarrow{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}
:
Let

q\in{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})
. Applying the regular
decomposition (
44
) to split any
u
in

{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})
as
u=\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u+\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u
, we define a functional

f_{q}
acting on
u
by

f_{q}(u)=-(\operatorname{div}q)(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u)+q(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u).

(84)

By the continuity of
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({i})}_{\mathrm{c}}}
,

\displaystyle|f_{q}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq\|\operatorname{div}q\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}\|\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u\|_{{\mathring{H}}^{1}}+\|q\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}\|\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u\|_{{\mathring{H}}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q\|_{{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})}\|u\|_{{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})},

so
f_{q}\in{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}
and

\|f_{q}\|_{{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}}\lesssim\|q\|_{{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})}.

(85)

Note that
f_{q}
is a distribution (which can be seen for instance by
the previously proved imbedding showing that
f_{q}
is in

{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})
). We now show
that the distribution
f_{q}
is identical to

q
. Indeed, by (
83a
) of
Lemma
5.2
, for any

\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\times\mathbb{{V}}
, the
definition (
84
) implies

f_{q}(\varphi)=q(\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}\varphi+\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}\varphi)=q(\varphi),

i.e.,
f_{q}=q
. Thus (
85
) implies that

\|f_{q}\|_{{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}}\equiv\|q\|_{{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})^{*}}\lesssim\|q\|_{{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})}
showing the stated continuous embedding.

Step 3
.
H(\operatorname{curl})^{*}\hookrightarrow{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div})
:
Writing

H(\operatorname{curl})
as
H(\operatorname{curl},\varOmega)
to explicitly indicate the domain, we
identify any given
f\in H(\operatorname{curl},\varOmega)^{*}
with the following
distribution in
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
,

\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})^{\prime}:\quad\tilde{f}(\varphi)=f(\varphi|_{\varOmega}),\quad\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3}).

By the Riesz representation theorem, there is a unique

u_{f}\in H(\operatorname{curl},\varOmega)
such that

f(v)=(\operatorname{curl}u_{f},\operatorname{curl}v)+(u_{f},v)
for all
v
in

H(\operatorname{curl},\varOmega)
. Let
\tilde{c}_{f}
and
\tilde{u}_{f}
denote the
extensions by zero of
L_{2}(\varOmega)
-functions
\operatorname{curl}u_{f}
and
u_{f}
by
zero to all
\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
. Then, for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})
,

\tilde{f}(\varphi)=f(\varphi|_{\varOmega})=(\tilde{c}_{f},\operatorname{curl}\varphi)_{L_{2}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})}+(\tilde{u}_{f},\varphi)_{L_{2}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})}.

This shows that the following identities hold in
\mathcal{{D}}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})
:

\displaystyle\tilde{f}=\operatorname{curl}\tilde{c}_{f}+\tilde{u}_{f},\qquad\operatorname{div}\tilde{f}=\operatorname{div}\tilde{u}_{f}.

These two identities give these corresponding bounds

\displaystyle\|f\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}

\displaystyle=\|\tilde{f}\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})}\leq\|\operatorname{curl}\tilde{c}_{f}\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})}+\|\tilde{u}_{f}\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{c}_{f}\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})}+\|\tilde{u}_{f}\|_{L_{2}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})}\lesssim\|u_{f}\|_{H(\operatorname{curl})},

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{div}f\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}

\displaystyle=\|\operatorname{div}\tilde{f}\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})}=\|\operatorname{div}\tilde{u}_{f}\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})}\lesssim\|\tilde{u}_{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})}=\|u_{f}\|_{L_{2}},

which prove that
\|f\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div})}\lesssim\|u_{f}\|_{H(\operatorname{curl})}=\|f\|_{H(\operatorname{curl})^{*}}
using the Riesz isometry.

Step 4
.
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div})\hookrightarrow H(\operatorname{curl})^{*}
:
In
exactly the same way the regularized Bogovskiĭ operators of
(
43
) give the regular decomposition
(
44
), the regularized Poincaré operators
of
[
16
]
show that there are continuous operators

\mathop{{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}:H(\operatorname{curl})\to H^{1}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}
and

\mathop{{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}:H(\operatorname{curl})\to H^{1}
such that any
u\in H(\operatorname{curl})
can be
decomposed into

u=\mathop{{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u+\operatorname{grad}\mathop{{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u.

(86)

Let
q\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div})
. Using the regular decomposition, we define a
functional

f_{q}(u)=-(\operatorname{div}q)(\mathop{{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u)+q(\mathop{{S}^{({u})}_{\mathrm{c}}})

where, on
the right hand side, the functional actions are duality pairings
between
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}
and
H^{1}
, well defined in view of (
20
). Hence

\displaystyle|f_{q}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq\|\operatorname{div}q\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}\|\mathop{{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u\|_{H^{1}}+\|q\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}\|\mathop{{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}u\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|q\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div})}\|u\|_{H(\operatorname{curl})}

Therefore
f_{q}
is in
H(\operatorname{curl})^{*}
and

\|f_{q}\|_{H(\operatorname{curl})^{*}}\lesssim\|q\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div})}.

(87)

Since we have already shown
that
H(\operatorname{curl})^{*}
is embedded into the subspace of

\mathbb{{R}}^{3}
-distributions in
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div})
, we conclude that

f_{q}\in\mathcal{{D}}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})^{\prime}
satisfies, for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}(\mathbb{{R}}^{3})
,

f_{q}(\varphi)=-(\operatorname{div}q)(\mathop{{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}\varphi)+q(\mathop{{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}\varphi)=q(\operatorname{grad}\mathop{{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{c}}}\varphi+\mathop{{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{c}}}\varphi)=q(\varphi),

i.e.,
f_{q}
and
q
coincide as distributions. Combined
with (
87
), we have thus shown that

\|q\|_{H(\operatorname{curl})^{*}}=\|f_{q}\|_{H(\operatorname{curl})^{*}}\lesssim\|q\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div})}.

Steps 1 and 2 prove the second identity of the lemma. Steps 3
and 4 prove the last identity of the lemma. The proofs of the
remaining identities are similar.
∎

In the next theorem we show that more dualities such as those in
Lemma
5.3
can be read off the diagrams with
and without boundary conditions. Let

\mathcal{L}:H^{1}\to{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\equiv(H^{1})^{*}
denote the Riesz map of
H^{1}

defined by

(\mathcal{L}w)(v)=(w,v)_{H^{1}}\equiv(\operatorname{grad}w,\operatorname{grad}v)+(w,v),\quad w,v\in{\mathring{H}}^{1},

(88)

where
(\cdot,\cdot)_{X}
denotes the inner product of
X
, and when
the subscript is absent, we interpret it as
L_{2}
products, as described
previously in (
8
). By the Riesz representation
theorem, recalling that our spaces are over the real field,
\mathcal{L}
is a
linear invertible isometry, so for any
q,r\in{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}
,

\displaystyle(q,r)_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{L}^{-1}q,\mathcal{L}^{-1}r)_{H^{1}}=r(\mathcal{L}^{-1}q),

where we have used (
88
) in the last step.
In particular, if a
v\in L_{2}\subset{{H}}^{-1}
is used in place of
r
above, the functional action becomes an
L_{2}
product and we obtain

(q,v)_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}=(\mathcal{L}^{-1}q,v),\qquad v\in L_{2}.

(89)

Note that
\mathcal{L}^{-1}q
is the result of solving a Neumann problem with

q
as the source. When
q
is a vector or matrix field, by

\mathcal{L}^{-1}q
we mean the respective vector or matrix field obtained by
component-wise application of
\mathcal{L}^{-1}
.

Theorem 5.4
.

The diagrams (
25
) and (
82
) are in
duality in the sense displayed below, where the second diagram has been
rearranged to easily display the duality (
*
) correspondences.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{H}}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2,\mathbb{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{H}}^{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{curl})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2}/\mathbb{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{curl})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{div})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\mathrm{dd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{div})/\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{H}}(\operatorname{div})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{H}}^{-1}(\operatorname{curl})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\mathrm{cd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\mathrm{cc}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{curl})/\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2,\mathbb{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}}(\operatorname{curl})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{2}/\mathbb{{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{div})/\mathcal{R}\mathcal{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(\operatorname{curl})/\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}/\mathcal{P}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}

(90)

Proof.

The strategy of this proof is the same as what was outlined in the
beginning of the proof of Lemma
5.3
. We
now focus on the spaces of matrix-valued functions.

Step 1.

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}^{*}\hookrightarrow H_{\mathrm{dd}}
:
Let

f\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}^{*}
. Then, by Riesz representation, there is a

g_{f}\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
such that
f(g)=(g_{f},g)_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}}
for all

g\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
. Expanding out the
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
inner product and using
(
89
), for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
,

\displaystyle f(\varphi)

\displaystyle=(g_{f},\varphi)_{{{H}}^{-1}}+(\operatorname{curl}g_{f},\operatorname{curl}\varphi)_{{{H}}^{-1}}+(\operatorname{inc}g_{f},\operatorname{inc}\varphi)_{{{H}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{L}^{-1}g_{f},\varphi)+(\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{curl}g_{f},\operatorname{curl}\varphi)+(\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{inc}g_{f},\operatorname{inc}\varphi).

Thus

f=\mathcal{L}^{-1}g_{f}+\operatorname{curl}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{curl}g_{f}+\operatorname{inc}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{inc}g_{f},

(91)

a sum of three terms, which are in
H^{1},L_{2}
, and
{{H}}^{-1}
,
respectively. Since
H^{1}\hookrightarrow L_{2}\hookrightarrow{{H}}^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{L}^{-1}g_{f}\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}+\|\operatorname{curl}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{curl}g_{f}\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}+\|\operatorname{inc}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{inc}g_{f}\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{L}^{-1}g_{f}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\operatorname{curl}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{curl}g_{f}\|_{L_{2}}+\|\operatorname{inc}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{inc}g_{f}\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{L}^{-1}g_{f}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{curl}g_{f}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{inc}g_{f}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle=\|g_{f}\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}+\|\operatorname{curl}g_{f}\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}+\|\operatorname{inc}g_{f}\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g_{f}\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}}=\|f\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}^{*}}

where we have used the Riesz isometry multiple times as well as the
continuity of the derivative
\partial_{i}:H^{m}\to H^{m-1}
for some
integers
m
(see e.g.,
[
24
, Theorem 1.4.4.6]
).
From (
91
) we also see that

\operatorname{div}f=\operatorname{div}\mathcal{L}^{-1}g_{f}
and

\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}f=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\mathcal{L}^{-1}g_{f}
, so
f
is indeed in

H_{\mathrm{dd}}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H_{\mathrm{dd}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|f\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}^{2}+\|\operatorname{div}\mathcal{L}^{-1}g_{f}\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}^{2}+\|\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\mathcal{L}^{-1}g_{f}\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}^{2}+\|\mathcal{L}^{-1}g_{f}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}=\|f\|_{{{H}}^{-1}}^{2}+\|g_{f}\|_{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}^{*}},

thus completing the proof of continuity of the embedding of
f
into
H_{\mathrm{dd}}
.

Step 2.

H_{\mathrm{dd}}\hookrightarrow\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}^{*}
:
Let
\sigma\in H_{\mathrm{dd}}
. Decomposing a
g\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}
using (
45
) as

g=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g+\operatorname{hess}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g
, we define a
linear functional
f_{\sigma}
acting on
g
as follows:

f_{\sigma}(g)=\sigma(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g)-(\operatorname{div}\sigma)(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g)+(\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma)(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}g).

By the continuity of
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({i})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}
(see Theorem
3.4
),

|f_{\sigma}(g)|\lesssim\|\sigma\|_{H_{\mathrm{dd}}}\|g\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}}
so

f_{\sigma}
is in
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}^{*}
and

\|f_{\sigma}\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}^{*}}\lesssim\|\sigma\|_{H_{\mathrm{dd}}}.

(92)

By the previous embedding, we know that
f_{\sigma}
is in
H_{\mathrm{dd}}
(in
particular in
