{{H}}^{-1}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
) and is therefore a distribution
on
\varOmega
. We claim that
f_{\sigma}
and
\sigma
are identical
distributions. Indeed, for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
,

\displaystyle f_{\sigma}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\sigma(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}\varphi)-(\operatorname{div}\sigma)(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}\varphi)+(\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma)(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}\varphi)

\displaystyle=\sigma(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}\varphi+\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}\varphi+\operatorname{hess}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cc}}}\varphi)=\sigma(\varphi)

where we have used (
83b
) and (
83c
) of
Lemma
5.2
. Combined
with (
92
), we have

\|\sigma\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}^{*}}\equiv\|f_{\sigma}\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}^{*}}\lesssim\|\sigma\|_{H_{\mathrm{dd}}},
thus completing the proof of
continuity of the embedding of
\sigma
into
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}^{*}
.

Step 3.

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}^{*}\hookrightarrow H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}
:
Let
f\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}^{*}
. By
the Riesz representation theorem, there is a unique

\tau_{f}\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
satisfying
f(\tau)=(\tau_{f},\tau)_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}
for
all
\tau\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
. Choosing

\tau=\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
, the definition of

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
-inner product and (
89
) imply

\displaystyle f(\varphi)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{L}^{-1}\tau_{f},\varphi)+(\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{div}\tau_{f},\operatorname{div}\varphi)+(\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau_{f},\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\varphi)

\displaystyle+(\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau_{f},\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\varphi).

Hence

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{-1}\tau_{f}-\operatorname{grad}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{div}\tau_{f}+\T\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{sym}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau_{f}

(93)

\displaystyle-\operatorname{grad}\operatorname{curl}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\tau_{f}.

Applying
\operatorname{div}\T
, the identity (
31
) shows that last term
vanishes and

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}\T f=\operatorname{div}\T\mathcal{L}^{-1}\tau_{f}-\operatorname{div}\T\operatorname{grad}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{div}\tau_{f}.

(94)

Moreover, applying
\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\T
to both sides of (
93
)
and using the identity (
28
),

\displaystyle\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\T f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\T\mathcal{L}^{-1}\tau_{f}.

(95)

Also,

\displaystyle\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\tau_{f}+\operatorname{inc}\operatorname{sym}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\T\tau_{f}.

(96)

Equations (
93
), (
94
),
(
95
) and (
96
) imply
that

\|f\|_{H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}}\lesssim\|\tau_{f}\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}=\|f\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}^{*}}

where we have used the isometry of
\mathcal{L}^{-1}
and

f\mapsto\tau_{f}
. This proves that

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}^{*}\hookrightarrow H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}
.

Step 4.

H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}\hookrightarrow\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}^{*}
:
Let
\tau\in H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}
. Define a linear functional
f_{\tau}
acting on any
\eta
in

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}
as follows:

f_{\tau}(\eta)=\tau(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\eta)+(\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\tau)(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\eta)-(\operatorname{div}\T\tau)(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({3})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\eta)-(\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\T\tau)(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\eta).

All the terms on the right are well defined duality pairings since

\tau,\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\tau,\operatorname{div}\T\tau,
and
\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{div}\T\tau,
have

{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}
components for any
\tau\in H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}
and since each

\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({i})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\eta
has
{\mathring{H}}^{1}
components per
Theorem
3.10
. The continuity of
\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({i})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}
asserted
by the same theorem gives

|f_{\tau}(\eta)|\lesssim\|\tau\|_{H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}}\|\eta\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}}.

(97)

Next, observe that for any

\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{T}}
, decomposing
\varphi
by
Theorem
3.10
into

\varphi=\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\varphi+\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\varphi+\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\varphi+\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({3})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\varphi,
and using (
83f
),
(
83g
) and (
83h
) of
Lemma
5.2
,

\displaystyle f_{\tau}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\tau(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\varphi)+\tau(\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\varphi)+\tau(\T\operatorname{dev}\operatorname{grad}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({3})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\varphi)+\tau(\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{def}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{cd}}}\varphi)

\displaystyle=\tau(\varphi).

Hence
f_{\tau}
and
\tau
are the same
distribution. Using (
97
),

\|\tau\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}^{*}}\equiv\|f_{\tau}\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}^{*}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}}
, which proves that
H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}\hookrightarrow\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}^{*}
is a
continuous embedding.

Step 5.

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}^{*}\hookrightarrow H_{\mathrm{cc}}
:
Denoting the Riesz
representative of any
f\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}^{*}
by
\sigma_{f}\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
, its
defining equation
f(\sigma)=(\sigma_{f},\sigma)_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}}
for all

\sigma\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
gives a formula for
f
as in the previous cases:

f=\mathcal{L}^{-1}\sigma_{f}-\operatorname{grad}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{div}\sigma_{f}+\operatorname{hess}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\sigma_{f}.

Then
\operatorname{curl}f=\operatorname{curl}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\sigma_{f}
is in
L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{{V}}

and
\operatorname{inc}f=\operatorname{inc}\mathcal{L}^{-1}\sigma_{f}
is in
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
. Hence
f
is in
H_{\mathrm{cc}}
and by Riesz isometries,

\|f\|_{H_{\mathrm{cc}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}^{*}}
.

Step 6.

H_{\mathrm{cc}}\hookrightarrow\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}^{*}
:
Let
g\in H_{\mathrm{cc}}
. We
will show that
g
can be identified with the functional
f_{g}

acting on
\sigma\in\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}
by

f_{g}(\sigma)=g(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma)+(\operatorname{curl}g)(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\sigma)+(\operatorname{inc}g)(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}).

By the regular decomposition result of
Theorem
3.7
, this implies that

|f_{g}(\sigma)|\lesssim\|g\|_{H_{\mathrm{cc}}}\|\sigma\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}}

(98)

and also using (
83d
) and (
83e
) of
Lemma
5.2
, we find that

f_{g}(\varphi)=g(\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({0})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\varphi+\operatorname{sym}\operatorname{curl}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({1})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\varphi+\operatorname{inc}\mathop{\mathring{S}^{({2})}_{\mathrm{dd}}}\varphi)=g(\varphi)

for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{{D}}(\varOmega)\otimes\mathbb{{S}}
, i.e.,
f_{g}
and
g

are the same distribution. Hence (
98
) shows that

\|g\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}^{*}}=\|f_{g}\|_{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}^{*}}\lesssim\|g\|_{H_{\mathrm{cc}}}

thus establishing the continuous embedding of
H_{\mathrm{cc}}
into

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}^{*}
.

To conclude the proof, note that Steps 1 and 2 prove that

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cc}}^{*}=H_{\mathrm{dd}}
. Steps 3 and 4 prove that
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}}^{*}=H_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}
and

\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{cd}\scalebox{0.6}{$\top$}}^{*}=H_{\mathrm{cd}}
. Steps 5 and 6 prove that
\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}_{\mathrm{dd}}^{*}=H_{\mathrm{cc}}
. To
establish the remaining nontrivial duality identities
in (
90
), it suffices to use the identities of
Lemma
5.3
and the fact that for any
Hilbert space
X
and its closed subspace
Z
, the dual of the
quotient space
(X/Z)^{*}
is isomorphic to the annihilator

Z^{\perp}=\{x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}:x^{\prime}(z)=0
for all
z\in Z\}
contained in

X^{\prime}
. For example, with
X=H(\operatorname{curl})
and
Z=\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}
, we
find that
(H(\operatorname{curl})/\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D})^{*}
is isomorphic to the annihilator of

\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}^{\perp}
contained in
H(\operatorname{curl})^{*}={\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}}(\operatorname{div})
, which is exactly
the same as
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div}),
i.e.,
\big(H(\operatorname{curl})/\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}\big)^{*}={\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div})
. Taking duals on both sides we obtain, due to the reflexivity
of Hilbert spaces,
{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathring{H}}$}}^{-1}_{\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D}}}(\operatorname{div})^{*}=H(\operatorname{curl})/\mathcal{N}\mathcal{D},
which is one of
the identities indicated in (
90
).
∎
